## 4. वरीयता वारंट (Warrant of Precedence) (पूर्वता क्रम, 1979 अधिसूचना)

*Frequently tested in arrange-order questions in UPPCS/UKPCS:*

1. **Rank 1**: **President of India**
2. **Rank 2**: **Vice-President of India**
3. **Rank 3**: **Prime Minister of India**
4. **Rank 4**: **Governors of States** (within their respective States)
5. **Rank 5**: **Former Presidents**
   - *Rank 5A*: **Deputy Prime Minister** (if any)
6. **Rank 6**: **Chief Justice of India (CJI)** and **Speaker of Lok Sabha** (*Both have equal rank!*)
7. **Rank 7**: **Cabinet Ministers of the Union**, **Chief Ministers of States** (within respective states), Deputy Chairman of NITI Aayog (former Planning Commission), **Leaders of Opposition in Rajya Sabha and Lok Sabha**
   - *Rank 7A*: **Holders of Bharat Ratna**
8. **Rank 8**: Ambassadors Extraordinary & Plenipotentiary, Chief Ministers (outside their respective states), Governors (outside their respective states)
9. **Rank 9**: **Judges of the Supreme Court of India**
   - *Rank 9A*: **Chief Election Commissioner (CEC)**, **Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG)**, Chairperson of UPSC
10. **Rank 10**: Deputy Chairman of Rajya Sabha, Deputy Speaker of Lok Sabha, Deputy Chief Ministers, Ministers of State of the Union
11. **Rank 11**: **Attorney General for India**, Cabinet Secretary, Lieutenant Governors (within their UTs).

---

## 5. संसदीय शर्तें, प्रस्ताव और कार्य प्रक्रिया (Parliamentary Terms, Motions & Working Procedure)

| Parliamentary Term | Core Meaning & Procedural Rules |
|---|---|
| **Question Hour** | First hour of every parliamentary sitting (11:00 AM to 12:00 PM):<br>• **Starred Questions**: Oral answers required; **supplementary questions CAN be asked** (green paper).<br>• **Unstarred Questions**: Written answers required; **NO supplementary questions allowed** (white paper).<br>• **Short Notice Questions**: Relates to matter of urgent public importance; asked with less than 10 days notice; answered orally (light pink paper). |
| **Zero Hour** | **Unique Indian parliamentary innovation (introduced in 1962)**; starts immediately after Question Hour (12:00 PM); members raise urgent matters without prior notice; **not mentioned in Rules of Procedure**. |
| **Adjournment Motion** | Introduced in **LOK SABHA ONLY** to draw attention to a definite matter of urgent public importance; requires support of **at least 50 members** to be admitted; sitting adjourned for debate ≥ 2.5 hours; involves censure of government. |
| **No-Confidence Motion** | Moved in **LOK SABHA ONLY** under Rule 198; requires support of **at least 50 members**; no specific grounds needed; if passed, Council of Ministers must resign. |
| **Censure Motion** | Moved in Lok Sabha against specific ministers or entire council for specific policies; requires specific grounds; if passed, government is not obligated to resign immediately but must seek confidence. |
| **Calling Attention Motion** | Indian parliamentary innovation (since 1954); calling attention of a Minister to a matter of urgent public importance and seeking an authoritative statement. Mentioned in Rules of Procedure. |
| **Cut Motions** | Moved during Budget debate (Lok Sabha only):<br>1. **Disapproval of Policy Cut**: Amount reduced to **Re 1** (shows complete policy rejection).<br>2. **Economy Cut**: Amount reduced by a specific lump sum.<br>3. **Token Cut**: Amount reduced by **Rs 100** to express specific grievance. |
| **Guillotine** | When time allotted for discussion on demands for grants concludes, all remaining undiscussed demands are put to vote immediately without debate. |
| **Lame-Duck Session** | Refers to the last session of the existing Lok Sabha after a new Lok Sabha has been elected. Those existing MPs who failed to get re-elected are called "Lame Ducks". |
| **Quorum** | Minimum number of members required to be present to conduct a sitting: **1/10th of total membership** of the House (55 in Lok Sabha, 25 in Rajya Sabha) per **Article 100(3)**. |

---

## 6. संसदीय समितियाँ (Parliamentary Committees) (तथ्य अवश्य याद रखें)

| Committee Name | Total Strength | Lok Sabha Members | Rajya Sabha Members | Term | Chairperson Rules & Key Functions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Public Accounts Committee (PAC)** | **22** | **15** | **7** | **1 year** | Set up first in **1921** (GOI Act 1919); scrutinizes **CAG Audit Reports**; elected by PR by STV; **Ministers CANNOT be members**; by convention since **1967**, Chairperson is appointed by the Speaker from the **Opposition**; known as **"Twin sister" of Estimates Committee** and **"Friend, Philosopher, and Guide"** of the CAG. |
| **Estimates Committee** | **30** | **30** | **0 (NO RS!)** | **1 year** | Set up in **1950** on recommendation of Finance Minister **John Mathai**; **LARGEST PARLIAMENTARY COMMITTEE**; all 30 members from Lok Sabha only; examines budget estimates and suggests alternative policies ("Continuous Economy Committee"); Chairperson appointed by Speaker from ruling party. |
| **Committee on Public Undertakings (COPU)** | **22** | **15** | **7** | **1 year** | Created in **1964** on recommendation of **Krishna Menon Committee**; examines reports of CAG on public undertakings; Chairperson appointed by Speaker from Lok Sabha members only. |
| **Departmentally Related Standing Committees (DRSCs)** | **31** (each) | **21** | **10** | **1 year** | Total **24 DRSCs** (16 work under Lok Sabha, 8 under Rajya Sabha); examine demands for grants, bills, and annual reports of respective ministries. |

---

## 7. संवैधानिक कार्यालय: आयु सीमा, कार्यकाल और नियुक्तियाँ (Constitutional Offices: Age Limits, Tenures & Appointments)

| Office / Position | Minimum Age Required | Retirement Age / Term of Office | Appointed By | Resignation Submitted To | Removal Procedure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **President of India** | **35 years** | **5 years** (eligible for re-election) | Electoral College (Elected MPs of LS/RS + Elected MLAs of States/UTs - Art. 54) | **Vice-President** | **Impeachment under Art. 61** (Quasi-judicial, 14 days notice, 2/3rd of total membership of both Houses; nominated MPs participate!) |
| **Vice-President of India** | **35 years** | **5 years** | Electoral College (All MPs of LS & RS, both elected & nominated - Art. 66; no MLAs!) | **President** | Resolution in Rajya Sabha by effective majority + agreed to by Lok Sabha (Art. 67) |
| **Governor of a State** | **35 years** | **5 years** (Holds office during **pleasure of President**) | President of India | **President** | Removed by President at any time without constitutional procedure |
| **Prime Minister / Union Minister** | **25 yrs** (LS) / **30 yrs** (RS) | No fixed tenure (during pleasure of President / confidence of LS) | राष्ट्रपति | **President** | Loss of majority in Lok Sabha |
| **Member of Parliament (Lok Sabha)** | **25 years** | **5 years** (unless dissolved earlier) | Direct Election (First-past-the-post) | **Speaker of Lok Sabha** | Disqualification under Art. 102 or 10th Schedule |
| **Member of Parliament (Rajya Sabha)** | **30 years** | **6 years** (Permanent body; **1/3rd members retire every 2 years**) | Indirect Election by elected MLAs (STV) | **Chairman of Rajya Sabha** | Disqualification under Art. 102 or 10th Schedule |
| **Member of Legislative Assembly (MLA)** | **25 years** | **5 years** | Direct Election | **Speaker of Assembly** | Disqualification under Art. 191 |
| **Member of Legislative Council (MLC)** | **30 years** | **6 years** (1/3rd retire every 2 years) | Indirect (MLAs 1/3, Local bodies 1/3, Teachers 1/12, Grads 1/12, Gov nominates 1/6) | **Chairman of Council** | Disqualification under Art. 191 |
| **Panchayat / Municipality Member** | **21 years** | **5 years** | Direct Election | पीठासीन अधिकारी | Disqualification under state municipal/panchayat laws |
| **Voter (Universal Adult Suffrage)** | **18 years** (lowered from 21 by 61st Amd) | — | Enrolled in electoral roll | — | Ineligibility under Art. 326 (unsound mind, crime) |
| **Supreme Court Judge / CJI** | No min age | **65 years** | President (Collegium recommendation) | **President** | Removed by Presidential order supported by special majority of both Houses for proved misbehaviour or incapacity (Art. 124(4)) |
| **High Court Judge / Chief Justice** | No min age | **62 years** (15th Amd raised from 60) | President (after consulting CJI, Governor, HC CJ) | **President** | Same manner and grounds as Supreme Court Judge |
| **Comptroller & Auditor General (CAG)** | No min age | **6 years or 65 years** (whichever earlier) | राष्ट्रपति | **President** | Same manner and grounds as Supreme Court Judge (Art. 148(1)) |
| **Chief Election Commissioner (CEC)** | No min age | **6 years or 65 years** | राष्ट्रपति | **President** | Same manner and grounds as Supreme Court Judge (Art. 324(5)) |
| **Attorney General for India** | Qualified as SC Judge | **No fixed term** (pleasure of President) | राष्ट्रपति | **President** | Removed by President at pleasure |
| **Advocate General for the State** | Qualified as HC Judge | **No fixed term** (pleasure of Governor) | Governor | **Governor** | Removed by Governor at pleasure |
| **UPSC Chairman / Member** | No min age | **6 years or 65 years** | राष्ट्रपति | **President** | Removed by President on ground of misbehaviour after inquiry by Supreme Court (Art. 317) |
| **State PSC Chairman / Member** | No min age | **6 years or 62 years** | **Governor appoints** | **Governor** | **REMOVED ONLY BY PRESIDENT** (Not Governor!) on SC inquiry under Art. 317 |
| **Central Vigilance Commissioner** | No min age | **4 years or 65 years** (whichever earlier) | President on 3-member panel (PM, Home Min, LoP LS) | **President** | Removed by President on SC inquiry |

---

## 8. उच्च-आवृत्ति लेखों को प्रतिदिन संशोधित करना चाहिए (High-Frequency Articles Must-Revise Daily)

### A. Fundamental Rights (Part III, Arts. 12–35)
- **Art. 12**: Definition of "State" (includes Union/State Governments, Parliament/Legislatures, Local authorities, other statutory authorities like LIC, ONGC).
- **Art. 13**: Laws inconsistent with or in derogation of Fundamental Rights shall be void (*Doctrine of Judicial Review*, *Doctrine of Severability*, *Doctrine of Eclipse*).
- **Art. 14**: Equality before law (British origin, negative concept) & Equal protection of the laws (American origin, positive concept).
- **Art. 15**: Prohibition of discrimination **ONLY** on grounds of religion, race, caste, sex, place of birth.
  - *Art. 15(4)*: Special provisions for SC/ST/OBC (1st Amd 1951).
  - *Art. 15(6)*: 10% EWS reservation in educational institutions (103rd Amd 2019).
- **Art. 16**: Equality of opportunity in matters of public employment.
  - *Art. 16(4)*: Reservation for backward classes not adequately represented.
  - *Art. 16(6)*: 10% EWS reservation in public employment (103rd Amd 2019).
- **Art. 17**: **Abolition of Untouchability** (Absolute right; enforced via Protection of Civil Rights Act 1955).
- **Art. 18**: Abolition of titles (Bharat Ratna/Padma awards are decorations, not titles — *Balaji Raghavan Case 1996*).
- **Art. 19**: Protection of **Six Democratic Freedoms**:
  1. *19(1)(a)*: Freedom of speech and expression (includes freedom of press, right to remain silent).
  2. *19(1)(b)*: Freedom to assemble peaceably and without arms.
  3. *19(1)(c)*: Freedom to form associations, unions, or co-operative societies (97th Amd).
  4. *19(1)(d)*: Freedom to move freely throughout the territory of India.
  5. *19(1)(e)*: Freedom to reside and settle in any part of the territory of India.
  6. *19(1)(g)*: Freedom to practice any profession, trade, or business.
  - *(Note: 19(1)(f) Right to acquire, hold, and dispose of property was REPEALED by 44th Amd 1978).*
- **Art. 20**: Protection in respect of conviction for offences:
  - *20(1)*: No ex-post-facto criminal law.
  - *20(2)*: No double jeopardy (cannot be prosecuted twice for the same offence).
  - *20(3)*: No self-incrimination (cannot be compelled to be a witness against oneself).
- **Art. 21**: **Protection of Life and Personal Liberty** (*Procedure Established by Law → Due Process of Law* via *Maneka Gandhi Case 1978*; encompasses Right to Privacy - *Puttaswamy 2017*, Right to clean environment, Right to livelihood, Right to sleep, Right to die with dignity / passive euthanasia - *Common Cause 2018*).
- **Art. 21A**: **Right to Free and Compulsory Education** for children aged 6 to 14 years (Inserted by **86th Amendment 2002**; RTE Act enacted in 2009, in force 1 April 2010).
- **Art. 22**: Protection against arrest and detention (production before magistrate within 24 hours; preventive detention max 3 months without advisory board approval).
- **Art. 23**: Prohibition of traffic in human beings and **Begar** (forced labour).
- **Art. 24**: Prohibition of employment of children **below 14 years in factories, mines**, or hazardous employment.
- **Arts. 25–28**: Freedom of Religion:
  - *Art. 25*: Freedom of conscience, free profession, practice, and propagation of religion.
  - *Art. 26*: Freedom to manage religious affairs.
  - *Art. 27*: Freedom from payment of taxes for promotion of any particular religion.
  - *Art. 28*: Freedom from attendance at religious instruction in state-funded educational institutions.
- **Arts. 29–30**: Cultural and Educational Rights of Minorities:
  - *Art. 29*: Protection of language, script, and culture of minorities.
  - *Art. 30*: Right of religious and linguistic minorities to establish and administer educational institutions.
- **Art. 32**: **Right to Constitutional Remedies**: Power of Supreme Court to issue 5 Writs (**Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Certiorari, Quo-Warranto**); Dr. B.R. Ambedkar called Article 32 the **"Heart and Soul of the Constitution"**.

> **NON-SUSPENDABLE RIGHTS:** Under **Article 359 (as amended by 44th Amd 1978)**, the President **CANNOT suspend the enforcement of Articles 20 and 21** even during a National Emergency!

### B. Directive Principles of State Policy (DPSP, Part IV, Arts. 36–51)
- **Art. 38**: State to secure a social order for the promotion of welfare of the people (minimize inequalities).
- **Art. 39(b) & (c)**: Equitable distribution of material resources of the community to subserve common good (39b); prevention of concentration of wealth (39c) — *Articles 39(b) and 39(c) take precedence over Articles 14 and 19 per Art. 31C!*
- **Art. 39A**: **Equal justice and free legal aid** (Added by 42nd Amd 1976; Legal Services Authorities Act 1987 / NALSA).
- **Art. 40**: **Organization of Village Panchayats** (Gandhian principle).
- **Art. 41**: Right to work, to education, and to public assistance in cases of unemployment, old age, sickness.
- **Art. 42**: Provision for just and humane conditions of work and **Maternity relief**.
- **Art. 43**: Living wage and decent standard of life for workers.
- **Art. 43A**: **Participation of workers in management of industries** (Added by 42nd Amd 1976).
- **Art. 43B**: Promotion of **Co-operative societies** (Added by 97th Amd 2011).
- **Art. 44**: **Uniform Civil Code (UCC)** for all citizens (**Goa** had Portuguese civil code; **Uttarakhand became the first state in post-independence India to pass a UCC bill in 2024!**).
- **Art. 45**: Provision for early childhood care and education for all children until they complete the age of **6 years** (Amended by 86th Amd 2002).
- **Art. 48**: Organization of agriculture and animal husbandry; prohibiting slaughter of cows, calves, and milch cattle.
- **Art. 48A**: **Protection and improvement of environment, and safeguarding of forests and wildlife** (Added by 42nd Amd 1976).
- **Art. 50**: **Separation of Judiciary from the Executive** in the public services of the State.
- **Art. 51**: Promotion of international peace and security.

### C. Union & State Executive / Legislature Comparisons
| Constitutional Matter | Union Article (President / Parliament) | State Article (Governor / State Legislature) | Key Distinction / Comparison |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Office of Head** | **Art. 52**: President of India | **Art. 153**: Governor of a State | 7th Amd 1956: Same person can be appointed Governor for two or more states |
| **Executive Power** | **Art. 53**: Vested in President | **Art. 154**: Vested in Governor | Governor is appointed by President (Canadian model); holds office during pleasure |
| **Pardoning Power** | **Art. 72**: President of India | **Art. 161**: Governor of a State | **President alone can pardon Death Sentences and Court Martial (Military) sentences.** Governor can suspend/remit, but cannot grant full pardon for death sentence |
| **Council of Ministers** | **Art. 74**: CoM to aid & advise | **Art. 163**: CoM to aid & advise | Governor has broader constitutional discretionary powers (Art. 163(2)) |
| **Appointment of PM/CM** | **Art. 75**: PM appointed by President | **Art. 164**: CM appointed by Governor | CoM size capped at **max 15%** of Lower House membership (91st Amd 2003); State min = 12 |
| **Top Legal Officer** | **Art. 76**: **Attorney General for India** | **Art. 165**: **Advocate General for the State** | AG has right to speak in both Houses of Parliament but **NO right to vote** (Art. 88) |
| **Joint Sitting** | **Art. 108**: Joint Sitting of Parliament | **NO PROVISION** for Joint Sitting in State Legislature! | Summoned by President; presided over by **Lok Sabha Speaker** (simple majority) |
| **Money Bill Definition** | **Art. 110**: Money Bill in Parliament | **Art. 199**: Money Bill in State Assembly | Speaker's decision is final; Rajya Sabha / Legislative Council has only **14 days** to suggest |
| **Annual Budget** | **Art. 112**: Annual Financial Statement | **Art. 202**: Annual Financial Statement | Word "Budget" is nowhere used in the Constitution! |
| **Ordinance Power** | **Art. 123**: President | **Art. 213**: Governor | Can only be promulgated when legislature is not in session; max lifespan: **6 weeks from reassembly** (absolute maximum lifespan = 6 months + 6 weeks) |
| **Assent to Bills** | **Art. 111**: President's Assent | **Art. 200**: Governor's Assent | Governor can reserve bill for consideration of President (**Art. 201**) |
| **Creation/Abolition of Council** | — | **Art. 169**: Legislative Council | State Assembly passes resolution by special majority → Parliament approves by simple majority |

## 9. संवैधानिक बनाम वैधानिक बनाम कार्यकारी निकाय (Constitutional vs Statutory vs Executive Bodies)

| Body Name | श्रेणी (Category) | Governing Article / Parent Act | Head / Composition | Appointed By | Removal / Tenure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Election Commission of India (ECI)** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 324** | Chief Election Commissioner + 2 Election Commissioners | President (per 2023 Act: panel of PM, LoP, Cabinet Min) | Same grounds and manner as Supreme Court Judge (6 yrs or 65 yrs) |
| **Union Public Service Commission (UPSC)** | **Constitutional** | **Arts. 315–323** | Chairman + Members (usually 9–11) | राष्ट्रपति | 6 yrs or 65 yrs; removed by President on SC enquiry under Art. 317 |
| **Finance Commission** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 280** | Chairman + 4 Members | President every 5th year | Recommends devolution of net tax proceeds between Union & States |
| **Comptroller & Auditor General (CAG)** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 148** | Single-member office | राष्ट्रपति | 6 yrs or 65 yrs; removed like a Supreme Court Judge; **"Guardian of the Public Purse"** |
| **Attorney General for India** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 76** | Single-member office | राष्ट्रपति | Holds office **during the pleasure of the President**; must be qualified to be an SC Judge |
| **Advocate General for the State** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 165** | Single-member office | Governor | Holds office **during the pleasure of the Governor**; qualified to be an HC Judge |
| **National Commission for SC (NCSC)** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 338** | Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson + 3 Members | राष्ट्रपति | 3 years tenure |
| **National Commission for ST (NCST)** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 338A** | Separated by 89th Amd 2003; Chairperson + 4 | राष्ट्रपति | 3 years tenure |
| **National Commission for BC (NCBC)** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 338B** | Accorded constitutional status by 102nd Amd 2018 | राष्ट्रपति | 3 years tenure |
| **GST Council** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 279A** | Chaired by Union Finance Minister; members: MoS Finance + State Finance Ministers | - | Voting weight: Centre = 1/3rd, States = 2/3rd; 3/4th majority needed to pass decisions |
| **Inter-State Council** | **Constitutional** | **Art. 263** | Set up in 1990 (**Sarkaria Commission**); chaired by Prime Minister | - | CMs of all states + 6 Union Cabinet Ministers |
| **National Human Rights Commission (NHRC)** | **Statutory** | **Protection of Human Rights Act (PHRA) 1993** | Retired CJI or Judge of SC (Chairperson) + Members | President on panel (PM, Speaker, Home Min, LoP LS, LoP RS, Dy Chairman RS) | 3 years or 70 years |
| **Central Vigilance Commission (CVC)** | **Statutory** | **CVC Act 2003** (set up 1964 on **Santhanam Committee**) | Central Vigilance Commissioner + max 2 Vigilance Commissioners | President on panel (PM, Home Minister, LoP LS) | **4 years or 65 years** (Unique tenure!) |
| **Central Information Commission (CIC)** | **Statutory** | **Right to Information (RTI) Act 2005** | Chief Information Commissioner + max 10 ICs | President on panel (PM, LoP LS, Union Cabinet Min) | Set by Central Govt or 65 yrs |
| **Lokpal of India** | **Statutory** | **Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act 2013** | Chairperson (CJI/SC Judge/eminent person) + max 8 Members | President on panel (PM, Speaker LS, LoP LS, CJI or nominee, eminent jurist) | 5 years or 70 years |
| **National Green Tribunal (NGT)** | **Statutory** | **NGT Act 2010** | Chairperson (retired SC judge or HC CJ) + Judicial/Expert members | Central Govt in consultation with CJI | 5 years; not eligible for reappointment |
| **NITI Aayog** | **Non-Constitutional (Executive Body)** | Created by **Union Cabinet Resolution on 1 Jan 2015** (replacing Planning Commission 1950) | Chairperson: Prime Minister; Vice-Chairperson + CEO + Ex-officio members | PM appoints | Think tank; promotes Cooperative & Competitive Federalism |

## 10. ऐतिहासिक संवैधानिक संशोधन (Landmark Constitutional Amendments) (संपूर्ण पीसीएस शीट)

| Amendment Act & Year | Prime Minister / Context | Landmark Provisions / Changes Made |
|---|---|---|
| **1st (1951)** | J.L. Nehru | Added **9th Schedule** to protect land reform laws from judicial review; placed reasonable restrictions on freedom of speech (public order, friendly relations with foreign states, incitement to an offence) |
| **7th (1956)** | J.L. Nehru | Implemented **States Reorganisation Act 1956** (Fazal Ali Commission); abolished Part A, B, C, D classification; created 14 States & 6 UTs; allowed common High Court for two or more states; allowed same person as Governor of multiple states |
| **24th (1971)** | Indira Gandhi | Affirmed Parliament's power to amend any part of the Constitution, including Fundamental Rights; made it **obligatory for the President to give assent** to a Constitutional Amendment Bill |
| **42nd (1976)** | Indira Gandhi (**Swaran Singh Committee**) | Known as the **"Mini-Constitution"**:<br>1. Preamble: Added words **"SOCIALIST, SECULAR, INTEGRITY"**<br>2. Added **Part IVA (Fundamental Duties, Art. 51A)** with 10 duties<br>3. Added **Part XIVA (Tribunals, Arts. 323A–323B)**<br>4. Added DPSPs: Arts. **39A** (Free legal aid), **43A** (Workers' participation), **48A** (Environment)<br>5. Transferred **5 subjects from State List to Concurrent List**: Education, Forests, Weights & Measures, Protection of Wild Animals & Birds, Administration of Justice |
| **44th (1978)** | Morarji Desai (Janata Party) | Undid emergency excesses of 42nd Amendment:<br>1. Replaced "Internal Disturbance" with **"Armed Rebellion"** in Art. 352<br>2. Mandated that President can declare National Emergency only on **written advice of the Cabinet**<br>3. **Right to Property deleted from Fundamental Rights** (Art. 19(1)(f) & Art. 31 deleted; made a legal right under **Art. 300A**)<br>4. Provided that **Articles 20 and 21 CANNOT be suspended** during emergency |
| **52nd (1985)** | Rajiv Gandhi | Added **10th Schedule (Anti-Defection Law)**; disqualified MPs/MLAs defecting from their political party |
| **61st (1988)** | Rajiv Gandhi | **Lowered voting age from 21 years to 18 years** for Lok Sabha and Legislative Assembly elections (amended **Art. 326**) |
| **69th (1991)** | P.V. Narasimha Rao | Conferred special status on Union Territory of Delhi as **National Capital Territory of Delhi (NCT)** with Legislative Assembly and 7-member Council of Ministers (inserted **Art. 239AA**) |
| **73rd (1992)** | P.V. Narasimha Rao | Accorded constitutional status to **Panchayati Raj Institutions (PRIs)**; inserted **Part IX (Arts. 243 to 243-O)** and **11th Schedule (29 subjects)**; 33% mandatory reservation for women |
| **74th (1992)** | P.V. Narasimha Rao | Accorded constitutional status to **Urban Local Bodies (Municipalities)**; inserted **Part IXA (Arts. 243P to 243ZG)** and **12th Schedule (18 subjects)** |
| **86th (2002)** | A.B. Vajpayee | 1. Inserted **Article 21A** (Right to free and compulsory education for children aged 6–14 as a Fundamental Right)<br>2. Substituted Article 45 (Early childhood care)<br>3. Added **11th Fundamental Duty** under **Art. 51A(k)** |
| **91st (2003)** | A.B. Vajpayee | 1. Capped the size of the Council of Ministers to **maximum 15%** of the total strength of Lok Sabha / State Legislative Assembly (minimum 12 in states)<br>2. **Deleted 1/3rd split provision** from 10th Schedule (only 2/3rd merger protected) |
| **99th (2014)** | Narendra Modi | Established National Judicial Appointments Commission (NJAC); **struck down as unconstitutional by Supreme Court in 2015** (*Fourth Judges Case*), restoring the Collegium system |
| **101st (2016)** | Narendra Modi | Introduced **Goods and Services Tax (GST)**; inserted **Art. 246A, 269A, and 279A** (GST Council chaired by Union Finance Minister); in force from **1 July 2017** |
| **102nd (2018)** | Narendra Modi | Accorded constitutional status to the **National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC)**; inserted **Articles 338B and 342A** |
| **103rd (2019)** | Narendra Modi | Provided **10% reservation for Economically Weaker Sections (EWS)** in educational admissions and civil posts; amended **Articles 15(6) and 16(6)** |
| **104th (2020)** | Narendra Modi | Extended reservation of seats for **SC and ST in Lok Sabha and State Assemblies for another 10 years (until 2030)**; **discontinued the nomination of 2 Anglo-Indian members** in Lok Sabha and 1 in Assemblies |
| **105th (2021)** | Narendra Modi | Clarified that State Governments maintain the power to prepare and maintain their own list of **Socially and Educationally Backward Classes (SEBCs / OBCs)** |
| **106th (2023)** | Narendra Modi | ***Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam***: Provides **33% reservation for women in Lok Sabha, State Legislative Assemblies, and NCT of Delhi Assembly** (inserted Arts. 330A, 332A, 239AA(2)(b)); effective after the next delimitation exercise |

## 11. पंचायती राज समितियाँ कालानुक्रमिक सीढ़ी (Panchayati Raj Committees Chronological Ladder)

| Committee Name | वर्ष (Year) | Landmark Recommendation / Impact |
|---|---|---|
| **Balwant Rai Mehta Committee** | **1957** | Recommended **Three-Tier Panchayati Raj System**:<br>1. Gram Panchayat (Village)<br>2. Panchayat Samiti (Block - executive body)<br>3. Zilla Parishad (District - advisory body)<br>**First implemented in Nagaur (Rajasthan) on 2 October 1959** by J.L. Nehru; second state was **Andhra Pradesh** |
| **K. Santhanam Committee** | **1963** | Panchayati Raj finances and budgetary powers |
| **Ashok Mehta Committee** | **1977** (Janata Govt) | Recommended **Two-Tier System**:<br>1. **Mandal Panchayat** (group of villages, pop. 15,000–20,000)<br>2. **Zilla Parishad** (District executive body)<br>Advocated official participation of political parties in elections |
| **G.V.K. Rao Committee** | **1985** (Planning Commission) | Critiqued bureaucratic takeover, describing PRIs as **"Grass without Roots"**; recommended strengthening Zilla Parishad and creating post of District Development Commissioner (DDC) |
| **L.M. Singhvi Committee** | **1986** (Rajiv Gandhi) | **FIRST TO STRONGLY RECOMMEND CONSTITUTIONAL RECOGNITION FOR PANCHAYATI RAJ**; advocated Nyaya Panchayats and financial autonomy |
| **P.K. Thungon Committee** | **1988** | Sub-committee of Parliamentary Consultative Committee; strongly urged **Constitutional status**, 3-tier system, fixed 5-year tenure, Zilla Parishad as main planning agency |
| **V.N. Gadgil Committee** | **1988** | Blueprint for the 73rd Amendment bill: 3 tiers, fixed 5-yr tenure, reservation for SC/ST/Women, State Election Commission, State Finance Commission |

## 12. सर्वोच्च न्यायालय के ऐतिहासिक निर्णय (Landmark Supreme Court Judgments) (कालानुक्रमिक तालिका)

| Case Name & Year | Key Issue / Doctrine Established |
|---|---|
| **Shankari Prasad Case (1951)** | Parliament can amend any part of the Constitution, including Fundamental Rights under Article 368. |
| **Golaknath Case (1967)** | 11-Judge bench; ruled that **Fundamental Rights are transcendental and sacrosanct**; Parliament CANNOT amend or take away Fundamental Rights. |
| **Kesavananda Bharati Case (1973)** | **13-Judge bench (largest ever, 7:6 verdict on 24 April 1973)**; upheld Parliament's power to amend the Constitution, BUT established the **"Basic Structure Doctrine"** — Parliament cannot alter the basic framework/features of the Constitution. |
| **Indira Nehru Gandhi v. Raj Narain (1975)** | Struck down 39th Amendment (Article 329A); held that **Free and Fair Elections and Judicial Review are part of the Basic Structure**. |
| **Maneka Gandhi Case (1978)** | Reinterpreted Article 21; replaced rigid "Procedure Established by Law" with **"Due Process of Law"**; procedure must be *just, fair, and reasonable*. Golden Triangle: Articles 14, 19, and 21 are interrelated. |
| **Minerva Mills Case (1980)** | Struck down clauses of 42nd Amendment; held that **limited amending power and judicial review are basic features**; Indian Constitution is founded on the bedrock of **balance between Fundamental Rights and DPSPs**. |
| **Waman Rao Case (1981)** | Reaffirmed Basic Structure Doctrine; set cutoff date of **24 April 1973** for applying Basic Structure scrutiny to the 9th Schedule. |
| **Shah Bano Case (1985)** | Upheld divorced Muslim woman's right to maintenance under Section 125 CrPC; urged enactment of a Uniform Civil Code. |
| **Indira Sawhney Case (1992)** (*Mandal Case*) | 9-Judge bench; upheld 27% OBC reservation; introduced **"Creamy Layer"** exclusion; capped **total reservations at 50%**; no reservation in promotions. |
| **S.R. Bommai Case (1994)** | 9-Judge bench; curbed misuse of **Article 356 (President's Rule)**; held that **Secularism is part of the Basic Structure**; floor test in Assembly is the only valid test of majority. |
| **Vishaka v. State of Rajasthan (1997)** | Formulated **Vishaka Guidelines** to prevent sexual harassment of women at workplaces (precursor to POSH Act 2013). |
| **I.R. Coelho Case (2007)** | 9-Judge bench; held that all laws inserted into the **9th Schedule after 24 April 1973 are subject to judicial review** if they violate Fundamental Rights forming part of the Basic Structure. |
| **Justice K.S. Puttaswamy Case (2017)** | 9-Judge bench unanimously held that the **Right to Privacy is a Fundamental Right** intrinsically protected under Article 21. |
| **Navtej Singh Johar Case (2018)** | Decriminalized consensual homosexual acts between adults by reading down **Section 377 of the Indian Penal Code (IPC)**. |
| **Jarnail Singh Case (2018)** | Held that the **"Creamy Layer" principle applies to SC/ST** when extending reservation in promotions. |
| **Janki Janak v. UOI (2022)** | Upheld the constitutional validity of the **103rd Constitutional Amendment (10% EWS reservation)** by 3:2 majority. |

---

## 13. राज्य पुनर्गठन कालक्रम (State Reorganization Chronology) (स्वतंत्रता के बाद का भारत)

1. **1953**: **Andhra State** created (first linguistic state, separated Telugu-speaking areas of Madras after 56-day fast unto death of **Potti Sreeramulu**; Kurnool capital).
2. **1956**: **States Reorganisation Act 1956** (Fazal Ali Commission with H.N. Kunzru and K.M. Panikkar); created **14 States and 6 Union Territories**.
3. **1960**: **Bilingual Bombay State bifurcated into Maharashtra and Gujarat** (Gujarat became the 15th state).
4. **1961**: **Dadra & Nagar Haveli** (10th Amd) and **Goa, Daman & Diu** (12th Amd) integrated via Operation Vijay.
5. **1962**: **Pondicherry** (Puducherry) integrated via 14th Amd (ceded by French in 1954).
6. **1963**: **Nagaland** created as 16th state (incorporating Naga Hills-Tuensang Area).
7. **1966**: **Punjab bifurcated** into Punjab (Punjabi) and **Haryana** (17th state, Hindi) on recommendation of **Shah Commission**; **Chandigarh** made common UT.
8. **1971**: **Himachal Pradesh** elevated from UT to 18th State.
9. **1972**: North-Eastern reorganization: **Manipur (19th), Tripura (20th), and Meghalaya (21st)** became full states; Mizoram and Arunachal Pradesh created as UTs.
10. **1975**: **Sikkim**: 35th Amd 1974 made it "Associate State" (10th Schedule added); **36th Amendment 1975 made Sikkim 22nd full State of India**.
11. **1987**: **Mizoram (23rd)**, **Arunachal Pradesh (24th)**, and **Goa (25th state)** elevated to full statehood.
12. **2000 (November)**: Three new states carved out:
    - **Chhattisgarh (26th state)**: 1 Nov 2000 (carved from MP).
    - **Uttarakhand (Uttaranchal, 27th state)**: **9 Nov 2000** (carved from Uttar Pradesh, 13 hill districts; renamed Uttarakhand on 1 Jan 2007).
    - **Jharkhand (28th state)**: 15 Nov 2000 (carved from Bihar on Birsa Munda's birth anniversary).
13. **2014 (2 June)**: **Telangana (29th state)** carved out of Andhra Pradesh (Sri Krishna Committee).
14. **2019 (31 October)**: **Jammu & Kashmir Reorganisation Act 2019**: State of J&K bifurcated into **two Union Territories: J&K (with assembly) and Ladakh (without assembly)**; total count: **28 States and 8 Union Territories**.
15. **2020 (26 Jan)**: Merged **Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu** into a single Union Territory.

> **From–to (do not stop at the birth year):** **Uttarakhand** as a state runs **9 Nov 2000 → present** (name **Uttaranchal** till **1 Jan 2007**). **Jammu & Kashmir as a State** ran till **31 Oct 2019**; **Art. 370** operative **1950 → 5/6 Aug 2019**. Goa was a UT **1961–1987**, then the **25th state**.

---

## 14. यूपी और उत्तराखंड राज्य विधानमंडल के त्वरित तथ्य (UP & Uttarakhand State Legislature Quick Facts)

| राज्य | Legislative Assembly (Vidhan Sabha) | Legislative Council (Vidhan Parishad) | Lok Sabha Seats | Rajya Sabha Seats | High Court & Benches |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **उत्तर प्रदेश** | **403 seats** (SC: 84, ST: 2) | **100 seats** (Bicameral!) | **80 seats** (Highest in India; SC reserved: 17, ST: 0) | **31 seats** (Highest in India) | **Allahabad High Court** (Established 1866 at Agra, shifted to Allahabad 1869; Lucknow Bench created 1948) |
| **उत्तराखंड** | **70 seats** (SC: 13, ST: 2) | **Unicameral** (No Council) | **5 seats** (Tehri, Garhwal, Almora - SC, Haridwar, Nainital-US Nagar) | **3 seats** | **Uttarakhand High Court** (Established 9 Nov 2000 at Nainital; shifting to Haldwani approved) |

## 15. रिट के पांच प्रकार (The Five Types of Writs) (अनुच्छेद 32 और 226)

*Power to issue prerogative writs: Supreme Court (**Article 32** — only for Fundamental Rights) and High Courts (**Article 226** — for Fundamental Rights AND ordinary legal rights; HC writ jurisdiction is wider than SC!)*

| Writ Name | Literal Latin Meaning | Purpose & Grounds for Issue | Against Whom Issued | Exceptions (Cannot be Issued Against) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Habeas Corpus** (बन्दी प्रत्यक्षीकरण) | **"To have the body of"** | Remedy against **unlawful detention or illegal custody**; court orders the detaining authority to produce the detained person before it to examine legality of detention | **Both Public Authorities and Private Individuals** | 1. Detention is lawful<br>2. Proceeding is for contempt of court/legislature<br>3. Detention is by competent court<br>4. Outside court's territorial jurisdiction |
| **Mandamus** (परमादेश) | **"We Command"** | Order issued to a public official/body directing them to **perform an official public duty** which they have failed or refused to perform | **Public officials, Public Corporations, Inferior Courts, Tribunals, Government** | 1. **Private individuals or private bodies**<br>2. Discretionary duties (not mandatory)<br>3. To enforce private contracts<br>4. **President of India or State Governors** (Art. 361)<br>5. Chief Justice of HC acting in judicial capacity |
| **Prohibition** (प्रतिषेध) | **"To Forbid"** | Issued by a higher court to a lower court/tribunal to **prevent it from exceeding its jurisdiction** or usurping a jurisdiction not vested in it; preventive in nature | **Judicial and Quasi-Judicial Authorities only** | Administrative authorities, legislative bodies, and private individuals/bodies |
| **Certiorari** (उत्प्रेषण) | **"To be Certified" / "To be Informed"** | Issued by a higher court to a lower court/tribunal to **quash an order already passed** in excess of jurisdiction, lack of jurisdiction, or violation of natural justice; both **preventive and curative** | **Judicial, Quasi-Judicial, and Administrative Authorities** (SC expanded to administrative bodies in 1991) | Legislative bodies and private individuals/bodies |
| **Quo-Warranto** (अधिकार-पृच्छा) | **"By What Authority or Warrant"** | Issued to enquire into the **legality of the claim of a person to a public office**; prevents illegal usurpation of a substantive public office | **Substantive Public Offices of a permanent nature created by statute/Constitution** | Ministerial offices (PM/Minister), private offices.<br>*(Can be sought by ANY interested person, not necessarily the aggrieved party!)* |

---

## 16. संविधान सभा समितियाँ एवं प्रारूप समिति के सदस्य (Constituent Assembly Committees & Drafting Committee Members)

| **Advisory Committee on Fundamental Rights, Minorities & Tribal Areas** | **सरदार वल्लभभाई पटेल** (**Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel**) | Apex advisory body with 5 sub-committees:<br>• *Fundamental Rights Sub-Committee*: **J.B. Kripalani**<br>• *Minorities Sub-Committee*: **H.C. Mukherjee**<br>• *North-East Frontier Tribal Areas*: **Gopinath Bardoloi**<br>• *Excluded & Partially Excluded Areas*: **A.V. Thakkar** |

## 17. केंद्र-राज्य संबंध और अंतर-राज्य नदी जल विवाद न्यायाधिकरण (Center-State Relations & Inter-State River Water Dispute Tribunals)

### A. Major Commissions on Center-State Relations
| Commission / Committee | वर्ष (Year) | Appointed By / State | Key Recommendations / Landmark Findings |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Administrative Reforms Commission (ARC-I)** | **1966** | Central Govt (Morarji Desai → K. Hanumanthaiah) | Recommended setting up of an **Inter-State Council under Article 263**; non-partisan Governors |
| **Rajamannar Committee** | **1969** | **Tamil Nadu Govt** (DMK, Dr. P.V. Rajamannar) | Strongly advocated State Autonomy; recommend immediate deletion of **Articles 356 and 365**; vesting residuary powers in States; abolishing All-India Services (IAS/IPS) |
| **Anandpur Sahib Resolution** | **1973** | Akali Dal (Punjab) | Demanded restriction of Central jurisdiction strictly to **Defence, Foreign Affairs, Currency, and Communications** |
| **West Bengal Memorandum** | **1977** | Left Front Govt (WB) | Demanded word "Union" be replaced by "Federation"; 75% of central revenues to states |
| **Sarkaria Commission** | **1983–1987** | Central Govt (Justice **R.S. Sarkaria**, B. Sivaraman, Dr. S.R. Sen) | **MOST INFLUENTIAL COMMISSION**:<br>1. **Article 356 must be used sparingly, only as a measure of last resort**<br>2. Institutionalize **Inter-State Council** under Art. 263 (established 1990)<br>3. Governor should be an eminent person from outside the state, detached from active politics, appointed after consulting CM<br>4. Opposed abolishing All-India Services, advocated strengthening them<br>5. Residuary legislative powers should remain with Centre (except taxation) |
| **Punchhi Commission** | **2007–2010** | Central Govt (Justice **M.M. Punchhi**) | 1. Localized emergency (targeted under Art. 355/356 without dissolving state assembly)<br>2. Governor's tenure: fixed 5 years, removal only through impeachment by State Legislature<br>3. Governor should not be Chancellor of state universities |

<div id="subject-08-indian-economy" class="subject-banner">
<span class="badge">Subject 08 · Economy</span>
<h1>SUBJECT 08: INDIAN ECONOMY</h1>
<h2>भारतीय अर्थव्यवस्था — दैनिक उच्च-प्राथमिकता तथ्य</h2>
<p>National Income Aggregates, 12 Five-Year Plans & Models, RBI Monetary Policy (Repo/SDF/CRR), CPI vs WPI, Fiscal Deficits, Finance Commission (15th/16th), and Poverty Committees.</p>
</div>

## 1. राष्ट्रीय आय समुच्चय और व्यापक आर्थिक सूत्र (National Income Aggregates & Macroeconomic Formulas)

| Concept / Metric | Formula / Technical Definition | Key Exam Traps & Prelims Triggers |
|---|---|---|
| **GDP at Market Price** | $GDP_{MP}= C + I + G + (X - M)$ | Total market value of all final goods & services produced **within the domestic territory** in 1 year. Foreigners earning in India included; Indians earning abroad excluded. |
| **GNP at Market Price** | $GNP_{MP}= GDP_{MP}+ NFIA$ ($GNP_{MP} = GDP_{MP} + NFIA$) | Measures income earned by **nationals/citizens** regardless of geographical location. $NFIA$ = Net Factor Income from Abroad (Factor income from abroad $-$ Factor income paid abroad). |
| **NNP at Market Price** | $NNP_{MP}= GNP_{MP}- \text{Depreciation}$ ($NNP_{MP} = GNP_{MP} - \text{Depreciation}$) | Deducts consumption of fixed capital (depreciation). **Depreciation is also called Capital Consumption Allowance**. |
| **NNP at Factor Cost (National Income)** | $NNP_{FC}= NNP_{MP}- \text{Net Indirect Taxes (NIT)}$ | Traditional official definition of **National Income**. Net Indirect Taxes (NIT) = Indirect Taxes $-$ Subsidies. |
| **Gross Value Added (GVA)** | $GVA_{Basic}= GVA_{Factor\ Cost}+ \text{Production Taxes}- \text{Production Subsidies}$ | Since 2015, India measures headline GDP as: **$GDP_{MP}= GVA_{Basic}+ \text{Product Taxes}- \text{Product Subsidies}$**. Base year changed to **2011–12**. |
| **Personal Disposable Income (PDI)** | $PDI = \text{Personal Income}- \text{Direct Taxes}- \text{Miscellaneous Fees/Fines}$ | Actual income available to households for consumption expenditure and savings. |
| **Real GDP vs Nominal GDP** | $\text{Real GDP}= \frac{\text{Nominal GDP}}{\text{GDP Deflator}} \times 100$ | Real GDP is calculated at **constant base year prices** (eliminates inflation impact). Nominal GDP is at current prices. |
| **GDP Deflator** | $\frac{\text{Nominal GDP}}{\text{Real GDP}} \times 100$ | Most comprehensive measure of inflation in an economy because it covers **all goods and services** produced, unlike CPI/WPI which use a fixed basket. |

> **Examiner Trap:** Transfer payments (old age pensions, unemployment allowance, student scholarships) are **excluded** from National Income because no corresponding production of goods or services occurs. Second-hand goods sales are also excluded (only broker commission is included).

---

## 2. पंचवर्षीय योजनाएँ (FYP) और नीति आयोग मास्टर मैट्रिक्स (Five-Year Plans (FYP) & NITI Aayog Master Matrix)

| Plan & Duration | Model / Architect | Core Objective / Strategy | Key Milestones & Exam Triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1st FYP (1951–56)** | **Harrod-Domar Model** | Agriculture, irrigation, price stability | Bhakra-Nangal, Hirakud, Damodar Valley dams launched; Target 2.1%, Achieved 3.6%. |
| **2nd FYP (1956–61)** | **P.C. Mahalanobis Model** | Rapid industrialisation, Heavy & Basic Industries | Steel plants at **Bhilai** (USSR), **Rourkela** (Germany), **Durgapur** (UK); Industrial Policy Resolution 1956. |
| **3rd FYP (1961–66)** | John Sandy & Sukhamoy Chakravarty | Self-reliant & self-generating economy | **Bokaro Steel Plant** (1964, USSR), FCI & CACP set up (1965). Plan failed due to 1962 China War, 1965 Pak War, 1965–66 Severe Drought. |
| **Plan Holiday (1966–69)** | Three Annual Plans | Green Revolution rollout | Devaluation of Indian Rupee (1966); High-Yielding Varieties (HYV) seeds introduced. |
| **4th FYP (1969–74)** | D.R. Gadgil Formula | Growth with stability & progressive self-reliance | **14 Major Commercial Banks Nationalised** (19 July 1969); Pokhran-I Nuclear Test (*Smiling Buddha*, 1974); 1971 Indo-Pak War. |
| **5th FYP (1974–79)** | D.P. Dhar Model | **Garibi Hatao** (Poverty Eradication) & Self-reliance | **20-Point Programme** (1975); Minimum Needs Programme (MNP); Terminated 1 year early in 1978 by Janata Govt. |
| **Rolling Plan (1978–80)** | Gunnar Myrdal concept | Annual revisions without fixed 5-year lock | Introduced by Janata Govt (Morarji Desai); ended when Congress returned in 1980. |
| **6th FYP (1980–85)** | Investment & Modernisation | Poverty eradication, economic & tech self-reliance | **NABARD established** (12 July 1982, Shivaraman Committee); **EXIM Bank** (1982); **6 more banks nationalised** (1980). |
| **7th FYP (1985–90)** | Pranab Mukherjee / Lakdawala | Food, Work & Productivity | Speed Post (1986); SEBI formed (1988); Jawahar Rozgar Yojana (JRY, 1989). |
| **Annual Plans (1990–92)** | Balance of Payments Crisis | Structural reforms & crisis stabilization | **LPG Reforms (1991)**: Liberalisation, Privatisation, Globalisation under P.V. Narasimha Rao & Dr. Manmohan Singh. |
| **8th FYP (1992–97)** | John W. Miller Model | Human Resource Development (employment, education) | India joined **WTO** (1 Jan 1995); National Stock Exchange (NSE) operationalised. |
| **9th FYP (1997–2002)** | Social Justice & Equality | Growth with Social Justice and Equity | 50 years of Indian Independence; Asian Financial Crisis backdrop. |
| **10th FYP (2002–07)** | Target 8% GDP Growth | Doubling per capita income in 10 years, reduce poverty by 5% | National Rural Employment Guarantee Act (**NREGA**, 2005); Bharat Nirman. |
| **11th FYP (2007–12)** | C. Rangarajan / Montek Singh | **Faster and More Inclusive Growth** | Right to Education (RTE Act, 2009); National Rural Livelihoods Mission (NRLM). |
| **12th FYP (2012–17)** | Final Five-Year Plan | **Faster, More Inclusive and Sustainable Growth** | Terminated; replaced by NITI Aayog 15-Year Vision, 7-Year Strategy & 3-Year Action Agenda. |

> **NITI Aayog (National Institution for Transforming India):** Formed on **1 January 2015** replacing the Planning Commission (15 March 1950). Non-constitutional, non-statutory extra-constitutional think tank. Chairperson: **Prime Minister**. Governing Council comprises all State Chief Ministers, UT Lt Governors/CMs. Operates on **Bottom-Up** approach and **Cooperative Federalism** (unlike top-down Planning Commission).

---

## 3. मौद्रिक नीति, आरबीआई उपकरण और धन आपूर्ति समुच्चय (Monetary Policy, RBI Instruments & Money Supply Aggregates)

### A. RBI Monetary Policy Instruments Master Table
| Instrument | Nature / Category | Operational Mechanism & Definition |
|---|---|---|
| **Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR)** | Quantitative / Direct | Percentage of Net Demand and Time Liabilities (NDTL) commercial banks **must keep with RBI in cash**. No interest paid by RBI on CRR. Governed by RBI Act, 1934 (Sec 42). |
| **Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR)** | Quantitative / Direct | Percentage of NDTL commercial banks **must maintain with themselves** in approved liquid assets (Cash, Gold, Govt Securities). Governed by Banking Regulation Act, 1949 (Sec 24). |
| **Repo Rate** | Quantitative / Indirect | Rate at which RBI lends short-term money to commercial banks against **government securities as collateral** (repurchase agreement). Primary policy rate. |
| **Reverse Repo Rate** | Quantitative / Indirect | Rate at which RBI borrows short-term funds from commercial banks against government securities. |
| **Standing Deposit Facility (SDF)** | Quantitative / Liquidity | Introduced in April 2022 under amended RBI Act (Sec 17). Allows RBI to absorb excess liquidity from commercial banks **without providing any collateral (G-Secs)**. Replaced Reverse Repo as floor of LAF corridor. |
| **Marginal Standing Facility (MSF)** | Quantitative / Liquidity | Penal rate at which scheduled commercial banks can borrow overnight funds from RBI by dipping into their SLR quota (up to prescribed limit). Ceiling of LAF corridor. |
| **Bank Rate** | Quantitative / Long-term | Rate at which RBI buys or rediscounts bills of exchange or commercial papers; benchmark for penal interest rates on non-compliance with reserve requirements. |
| **Open Market Operations (OMO)** | Quantitative / Market | Outright purchase and sale of Government Securities (G-Secs) by RBI in open market to inject or absorb durable liquidity. |
| **Moral Suasion, Credit Rationing, Margin Requirements** | Qualitative / Selective | Directives to banks to channel credit to specific priority sectors, control speculative borrowing, and regulate loan-to-value (LTV) ratios. |

### B. Money Supply Measures (Monetary Aggregates)
- **Reserve Money ($M_0$ / High Powered Money):** Currency in Circulation $+$ Bankers' deposits with RBI $+$ 'Other' deposits with RBI.
- **Narrow Money ($M_1$):** Currency with Public $+$ Demand Deposits with Banking System (Current & Savings Account - CASA) $+$ 'Other' deposits with RBI. *(Most liquid aggregate).*
- **$M_2$:** $M_1 +$ Post Office Savings Bank deposits.
- **Broad Money ($M_3$):** $M_1 +$ Time Deposits (Fixed/Recurring Deposits) with the banking system. *(Most commonly used indicator of money supply in India).*
- **$M_4$:** $M_3 +$ All deposits with Post Office Savings Organisation (excluding National Savings Certificates / NSC).
- **Liquidity Hierarchy:** $M_1 > M_2 > M_3 > M_4$.

### C. Monetary Policy Committee (MPC)
- Statutory body constituted under **Section 45ZB of RBI Act, 1934** (amended by Finance Act, 2016).
- **6 Members:** 3 from RBI (RBI Governor as ex-officio Chairperson, Deputy Governor in charge of monetary policy, 1 RBI officer) $+$ 3 external members appointed by Central Government.
- **Inflation Target:** Central Govt in consultation with RBI sets retail inflation target (CPI headline) at **4% with a tolerance band of $\pm 2\%$** (i.e., 2% to 6%).
- Meets at least **4 times a year**. Quorum is 4 members. Each member has 1 vote; RBI Governor has a **casting vote** in case of a tie.

---

## 4. मुद्रास्फीति सूचकांक: सीपीआई बनाम डब्ल्यूपीआई मास्टर तुलना (Inflation Indices: CPI vs WPI Master Comparison)

| Parameter | Wholesale Price Index (WPI) | Consumer Price Index (CPI - Combined) |
|---|---|---|
| **Publishing Authority** | Office of Economic Adviser (DPIIT), Ministry of Commerce & Industry | National Statistical Office (NSO), Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation (MoSPI) |
| **Base Year** | **2011–12** (Revised from 2004–05 in 2017) | **2012** |
| **Official Headline Anchor** | Used for wholesale trade transactions | **Official headline anchor for RBI Monetary Policy** (since Urjit Patel Committee, 2014) |
| **Services Included?** | **NO services included** (Goods only) | **YES, Services are included** (Housing, Transport, Education, Medical, etc.) |
| **Number of Commodities** | **697 items** | 448 items (Rural), 460 items (Urban) |
| **Highest Weight Group** | **Manufactured Products (64.23%)** > Primary Articles (22.62%) > Fuel & Power (13.15%) | **Food and Beverages (45.86%)** > Misc/Services (28.32%) > Housing (10.07%) |
| **Price Point Measured** | First point of bulk commercial transaction | Retail consumer purchase point |

---

## 5. सार्वजनिक वित्त, घाटा और वित्त आयोग (Public Finance, Deficits & Finance Commission)

### A. Deficit Formulas Master Sheet
1. **Fiscal Deficit (FD):** $\text{Total Expenditure}- \text{Total Receipts (excluding borrowings)}$. It represents the total borrowing requirements of the government from all sources in that year.
2. **Revenue Deficit (RD):** $\text{Revenue Expenditure}- \text{Revenue Receipts}$. Indicates that the government is borrowing to finance day-to-day consumption rather than capital asset creation.
3. **Effective Revenue Deficit (ERD):** $\text{Revenue Deficit}- \text{Grants for creation of capital assets}$. Introduced in Union Budget 2011–12.
4. **Primary Deficit (PD):** $\text{Fiscal Deficit}- \text{Interest Payments}$. Measures the current year's fiscal stance excluding the burden of past accumulated debt interest.

### B. Finance Commission (Article 280) Master Matrix
- Quasi-judicial body constituted by the **President every 5 years** under Article 280.
- Recommends devolution of net proceeds of taxes between Union and States (Vertical devolution) and allocation among States (Horizontal devolution).
- **1st FC:** K.C. Neogy (1951)
- **13th FC:** Dr. Vijay Kelkar (recommending GST framework)
- **14th FC:** Dr. Y.V. Reddy (increased States' share sharply from 32% to **42%**)
- **15th FC:** **N.K. Singh** (2020–26): Devolution share set at **41%** (1% adjusted for newly created UTs of J&K and Ladakh). Criteria weights: Income Distance (45%), Population 2011 (15%), Area (15%), Forest & Ecology (10%), Demographic Performance (12.5%), Tax Effort (2.5%).
- **16th FC:** Constituted on 31 Dec 2023 under chairmanship of **Dr. Arvind Panagariya** (former Vice Chairman of NITI Aayog).

---

## 6. बैंकिंग और वित्तीय क्षेत्र के मील के पत्थर (Banking & Financial Sector Milestones)

- **1935 (1 April):** RBI established on recommendations of **Hilton Young Commission (1926)** under RBI Act, 1934. Initial capital ₹5 crore; nationalised on **1 January 1949**.
- **1955:** Imperial Bank of India (formed in 1921 by amalgamating Presidency Banks of Bengal, Bombay, Madras) nationalised and renamed **State Bank of India (SBI)** on recommendation of All India Rural Credit Survey Committee (AD Gorwala).
- **1969 (19 July):** **14 Major Commercial Banks** (with deposits $\ge ₹50$ crore) nationalised by Indira Gandhi Govt via Banking Companies (Acquisition and Transfer of Undertakings) Ordinance.
- **1975 (2 October):** First Regional Rural Bank (**Prathama Bank**, Moradabad, UP) established on recommendations of **M. Narasimham Working Group**.
- **1980 (15 April):** **6 More Commercial Banks** (with deposits $\ge ₹200$ crore) nationalised.
- **1982 (12 July):** **NABARD** established under NABARD Act, 1981 on recommendation of **B. Sivaraman Committee**.
- **1988:** SEBI set up as non-statutory body; granted statutory powers via **SEBI Act, 1992** on 30 January 1992.
- **Priority Sector Lending (PSL) Targets:** Total PSL target for Domestic Scheduled Commercial Banks and Foreign Banks with $\ge 20$ branches is **40% of Adjusted Net Bank Credit (ANBC)**. Sub-targets: Agriculture (18%, within which 10% for Small & Marginal Farmers), Micro Enterprises (7.5%), Weaker Sections (12%). For Regional Rural Banks (RRBs) and Small Finance Banks (SFBs), total PSL target is **75% of ANBC**.

---

## 7. कृषि क्रांतियाँ, योजनाएँ और एमएसपी (Agricultural Revolutions, Schemes & MSP)

### A. Colour Revolutions in Indian Agriculture
| Colour Revolution | Associated Sector / Commodity | Pioneer / Father in India |
|---|---|---|
| **Green Revolution** | Food Grains (Wheat & Rice, HYV seeds) | **M.S. Swaminathan** (Norman Borlaug globally) |
| **White Revolution (Operation Flood)** | Milk & Dairy Development (1970) | **Dr. Verghese Kurien** (Amul / NDDB Anand) |
| **Blue Revolution** | Fisheries & Aquaculture | Dr. Hiralal Chaudhuri & Dr. Arun Krishnan |
| **Yellow Revolution** | Oilseeds (Mustard, Sunflower, Soyabean) | Sam Pitroda |
| **Golden Revolution** | Horticulture, Honey, Fruits | Nirpakh Tutej |
| **Golden Fibre Revolution** | **Jute** production | - |
| **Silver Revolution** | Eggs & Poultry | Indira Gandhi |
| **Silver Fibre Revolution** | **Cotton** production | - |
| **Pink Revolution** | Onion, Pharmaceuticals, Prawn | Durgesh Patel |
| **Red Revolution** | Meat & Tomato production | Vishal Tewari |
| **Round Revolution** | **Potato** production | - |
| **Grey Revolution** | Fertilizers | - |
| **Brown Revolution** | Leather, Non-conventional energy, Cocoa | - |
| **Rainbow Revolution** | Integrated development of all agri sectors | Announced in Agri Policy 2000 |

### B. Minimum Support Price (MSP) Mechanism
- Announced by Central Government on recommendations of **CACP (Commission for Agricultural Costs and Prices)** before sowing season.
- Covers **22 mandated crops** $+$ Fair and Remunerative Price (FRP) for **Sugarcane**:
  - 7 Cereals (Paddy, Wheat, Maize, Jowar, Bajra, Ragi, Barley)
  - 5 Pulses (Gram, Arhar/Tur, Moong, Urad, Masur)
  - 8 Oilseeds (Groundnut, Rapeseed/Mustard, Soyabean, Sesamum, Sunflower, Safflower, Nigerseed, Copra)
  - Raw Cotton, Raw Jute, De-husked Coconut
- **Cost Concepts used by CACP:**
  - $A2$: Direct paid-out costs on seeds, fertilizers, pesticides, hired labour, fuel, irrigation.
  - $A2 + FL$: $A2 +$ Imputed value of unpaid family labour.
  - $C2$: Comprehensive cost ($A2 + FL +$ rental value of owned land $+$ interest on owned capital assets).
  - Central Govt formula since Union Budget 2018–19: MSP set at **at least 1.5 times ($150\%$) of $A2 + FL$**.

---

## 8. गरीबी आकलन समितियाँ और बहुआयामी गरीबी (Poverty Estimation Committees & Multidimensional Poverty)

| Committee & Year | Consumption Basket / Criteria Used | Poverty Line Benchmark (Monthly / Daily) |
|---|---|---|
| **Y.K. Alagh (1979)** | Minimum Nutritional Calorie intake | **2,400 kcal/day** in Rural areas; **2,100 kcal/day** in Urban areas. |
| **D.T. Lakdawala (1993)** | Calorie norms linked with state-specific price indices (CPI-AL for rural, CPI-IW for urban) | State-specific poverty lines; rejected uniform national basket. |
| **Suresh Tendulkar (2009)** | Shifted from calories to mixed reference basket including **Health, Education, Clothing, Footwear** | Set per capita daily consumption at **₹27.2 in rural** and **₹33.3 in urban** areas (₹816/month rural, ₹1000/month urban at 2011–12 prices). National poverty rate: **21.9%** (2011–12). |
| **C. Rangarajan (2014)** | Independent nutritional norm (2,155 kcal rural, 2,090 kcal urban + protein/fat) + non-food expenses | Set per capita daily consumption at **₹32 in rural** and **₹47 in urban** areas (₹972/month rural, ₹1407/month urban). National poverty rate: **29.5%** (2011–12). |

> **Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI) by NITI Aayog:**  
> Follows Alkire-Foster methodology. **3 Dimensions and 12 Indicators** (each dimension weighted equally at $1/3$):
> 1. **Health (1/3):** Nutrition (1/6), Child & Adolescent Mortality (1/12), Maternal Health (1/12).
> 2. **Education (1/3):** Years of Schooling (1/6), School Attendance (1/6).
> 3. **Standard of Living (1/3):** Cooking Fuel, Sanitation, Drinking Water, Electricity, Housing, Assets, Bank Account (each 1/21).

---

## 9. अंतर्राष्ट्रीय वित्तीय संस्थान और वैश्विक आर्थिक रिपोर्ट (International Financial Institutions & Global Economic Reports)

| Institution / Group | Established | Headquarters | Key Publications / Reports |
|---|---|---|---|
| **International Monetary Fund (IMF)** | 1944 (Bretton Woods) | **Washington D.C.** | **World Economic Outlook (WEO)**, **Global Financial Stability Report (GFSR)**, Fiscal Monitor |
| **World Bank (IBRD + IDA)** | 1944 (Bretton Woods) | **Washington D.C.** | **Global Economic Prospects (GEP)**, World Development Report (WDR) |
| **World Economic Forum (WEF)** | 1971 (Klaus Schwab) | **Cologny/Geneva (Switzerland)** | **Global Competitiveness Report**, **Global Gender Gap Report**, Global Risks Report, Energy Transition Index |
| **World Trade Organisation (WTO)** | 1 Jan 1995 (Marrakesh) | **Geneva (Switzerland)** | World Trade Report |
| **Asian Development Bank (ADB)** | 1966 | **Mandaluyong, Manila (Philippines)** | Asian Development Outlook (ADO) |
| **Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank (AIIB)** | 2016 | **Beijing (China)** | China largest shareholder, India 2nd largest shareholder |
| **New Development Bank (NDB / BRICS Bank)** | 2014 (Fortaleza) | **Shanghai (China)** | First President: K.V. Kamath (India) |
| **UNDP** | 1965 | **New York (USA)** | **Human Development Report (HDR)** — HDI, IHDI, GII, MPI |

> **Special Drawing Rights (SDR - Paper Gold):** International reserve asset created by the IMF in 1969. The SDR basket includes **5 currencies**: US Dollar, Euro, Chinese Renminbi (Yuan - added in 2016), Japanese Yen, and British Pound Sterling.

<h1>SUBJECT 09: CENSUS & URBANISATION (INDIA & UP)</h1>
<h2>जनगणना एवं नगरीकरण — दैनिक उच्च-प्राथमिकता तथ्य</h2>
<p>Census 2011 National Indicators, State Top & Bottom Extremes, SC & ST Demographics, Religious Hierarchy, Uttar Pradesh District Extremes, and Urbanisation Rates in India & UP.</p>
</div>

## 1. भारतीय जनगणना इतिहास और कानूनी ढांचा (Indian Census History & Legal Framework)

| Milestone / Parameter | Year / Detail | Historical / Legal Context |
|---|---|---|
| **First Census in Modern India** | **1872** | Conducted non-synchronously under **Lord Mayo**. |
| **First Synchronous Decennial Census** | **1881** | Conducted across all India under Viceroy **Lord Ripon** (Census Commissioner: **W.C. Plowden**). |
| **The Year of the Great Divide** | **1921** | The only census decade in Indian history showing a **negative population growth rate (-0.31%)** due to 1918 Spanish Flu pandemic, plague, and severe famine. |
| **First Census of Independent India** | **1951** | Census Commissioner: **R.A. Gopalaswami**. Total population: 361 million; sex ratio: 946. |
| **Census Act, 1948** | Enacted under Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel | Permanent statutory basis; census figures are strictly confidential and inadmissible in courts as evidence. |
| **Nodal Authority** | Registrar General and Census Commissioner of India (RGCCI) | Under **Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA)**, New Delhi. |
| **2011 Census Edition** | **15th Census of India** (7th after Independence) | Motto: *"Our Census, Our Future"*; Mascot: Woman Enumerator; Commissioner: **Dr. C. Chandramouli**. |

---

## 2. जनगणना 2011 मास्टर मैट्रिक्स: राष्ट्रीय संकेतक (Census 2011 Master Matrix: National Indicators)

| जनसांख्यिकीय संकेतक | Census 2001 | Census 2011 | Absolute / Percentage Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| **कुल जनसंख्या** | 1,028.7 million | **1,210.85 million** (~121.08 Cr) | $+182.1$ million (+17.7%) |
| **Male Population** | 532.2 million (51.74%) | **623.27 million (51.54%)** | Male proportion decreased slightly |
| **Female Population** | 496.5 million (48.26%) | **587.58 million (48.46%)** | Female proportion increased |
| **दशकीय विकास दर (2001-11)** | 21.54% | **17.70%** | Decreased by **3.84 percentage points** |
| **Annual Exponential Growth Rate** | 1.97% | **1.64%** | Decelerating trend |
| **Population Density (persons/sq km)** | 325 | **382** | Increased by **+57 persons/sq km** |
| **Overall Sex Ratio (females/1000 males)** | 933 | **943** | Improved by **+10 points** |
| **Child Sex Ratio (0–6 years)** | 927 | **918** | **Declined by -9 points** (Critical alarm zone) |
| **Child Population (0–6 years) share** | 15.93% | **13.59%** | Reflects falling fertility rates |
| **Overall Literacy Rate** | 64.83% | **73.00%** (some official reports 74.04%) | Increased by **+8.17 percentage points** |
| **Male Literacy Rate** | 75.26% | **80.90%** (or 82.14%) | Improved |
| **Female Literacy Rate** | 53.67% | **64.60%** (or 65.46%) | Faster gain than males (+10.93%) |
| **Gender Gap in Literacy** | 21.59% | **16.30%** (or 16.68%) | Narrowed by **5.29 percentage points** |
| **Urban Population Share** | 27.81% | **31.16%** | Increased by $+3.35$ percentage points |
| **Rural Population Share** | 72.19% | **68.84%** | Decreased proportionally |

## 4. अनुसूचित जाति (एससी) और अनुसूचित जनजाति (एसटी) जनसांख्यिकी (Scheduled Castes (SC) and Scheduled Tribes (ST) Demographics)

### A. Scheduled Castes (SC) — Census 2011
- Total SC Population in India: **201.4 million** (**16.6%** of total population).
- **Highest SC Population (Absolute):** **Uttar Pradesh** (41.35 million, accounts for 20.5% of total Indian SCs) > West Bengal (21.46M) > Bihar (16.57M).
- **Highest SC Percentage (% of State Population):** **Punjab** (**31.9%**) > Himachal Pradesh (25.2%) > West Bengal (23.5%) > Uttar Pradesh (20.7%).
- **States/UTs with NO Scheduled Castes:** **Nagaland, Mizoram, Lakshadweep, Andaman & Nicobar Islands**.

### B. Scheduled Tribes (ST) — Census 2011
- Total ST Population in India: **104.3 million** (**8.6%** of total population).
- **Highest ST Population (Absolute):** **Madhya Pradesh** (15.31 million, 14.7% of all Indian STs) > Maharashtra (10.51M) > Odisha (9.59M).
- **Highest ST Percentage (% of State/UT):** **Lakshadweep** (**94.8%**) and **Mizoram** (**94.4%**) > Nagaland (86.5%) > Meghalaya (86.1%).
- **States/UTs with NO Scheduled Tribes:** **Punjab, Haryana, Chandigarh, Delhi, Puducherry**.

## 7. उत्तराखंड जनगणना 2011 की मुख्य बातें (Uttarakhand Census 2011 Highlights)

- **Total Population:** **10,086,292** (~1.01 Crore, 0.83% of India).
- **Decadal Growth Rate:** **18.81%** (Plains growing rapidly: US Nagar 33.4%, Haridwar 30.6%; Hill districts showing negative growth: **Almora -1.28%**, **Pauri Garhwal -1.41%** - ghost villages phenomenon).
- **Population Density:** **189 persons/sq km** (Haridwar highest at 801; Uttarkashi lowest at 41).
- **Overall Sex Ratio:** **963 females/1000 males** (Almora highest at 1,139; Haridwar lowest at 880).
- **Child Sex Ratio (0–6 years):** **890** (Pithoragarh lowest at 816).
- **Overall Literacy Rate:** **78.82%** (Male 87.40%, Female 70.01%). Highest literacy: **Dehradun (84.25%)**; Lowest: **Haridwar (73.43%)**.

<div id="subject-10-uttar-pradesh-special-gk" class="subject-banner">
<span class="badge">Subject 10 · UP Special GK</span>
<h1>SUBJECT 10: UTTAR PRADESH SPECIAL GK</h1>
<h2>उत्तर प्रदेश विशेष सामान्य ज्ञान — दैनिक उच्च-प्राथमिकता तथ्य</h2>
<p>Geographic Boundaries & 4 Extremes, Drainage & Rivers, Dudhwa NP & 4 Tiger Reserves, 10 Ramsar Sites, 9 Agro-Climatic Zones, Minerals, 1857 Revolt, Gharanas, and 75 Districts ODOP.</p>
</div>

## 1. उत्तर प्रदेश भौगोलिक प्रोफ़ाइल और सीमा मैट्रिक्स (Uttar Pradesh Geographical Profile & Boundary Matrix)

| Geographical Dimension | Exact Specification & Measurement | High-Yield Prelims Anchor |
|---|---|---|
| **अक्षांशीय विस्तार** | $23^{\circ}52'\text{ N}$ to $30^{\circ}24'\text{ N}$ ($23^{\circ} 52'\text{ N}$ to $30^{\circ} 24'\text{ N}$) | Latitudinal span: $6^{\circ}32'$ |
| **अनुदैर्ध्य विस्तार** | $77^{\circ}05'\text{ E}$ to $84^{\circ}38'\text{ E}$ ($77^{\circ} 05'\text{ E}$ to $84^{\circ} 38'\text{ E}$) | Longitudinal span: $7^{\circ}33'$ |
| **Standard Time Meridian** | **$82^{\circ}30'\text{ E}$ (IST)** passes through **Mirzapur (near Prayagraj)** | Calculates Indian Standard Time (GMT $+5:30$) |
| **Total Geographic Area** | **240,928 sq km** (7.33% of India's total area) | **4th Largest State** by area (Rajasthan > MP > Maharashtra > UP) |
| **East-to-West Length** | **650 km** | Extreme length |
| **North-to-South Width** | **240 km** | Narrower width |
| **Number of Neighboring Entities** | **8 States + 1 UT (Delhi) + 1 International (Nepal)** | Shares borders with the **maximum number of states** in India |

### Boundary Touch-Points Master Grid
- **Northern Boundary:** Uttarakhand & Himachal Pradesh (Himachal touches only **Saharanpur** - shortest border).
- **North-Eastern Boundary:** **Nepal** (Touches **7 Districts:** Pilibhit, Lakhimpur Kheri, Bahraich, Shrawasti, Balrampur, Siddharthnagar, Maharajganj — 579 km border).
- **Western Boundary:** Haryana, Delhi (UT), Rajasthan.
  - Touches Haryana: Saharanpur, Shamli, Baghpat, Gautam Buddha Nagar, Aligarh, Mathura (6 districts; separated by Yamuna river).
  - Touches Delhi: Ghaziabad & Gautam Buddha Nagar.
  - Touches Rajasthan: **Mathura & Agra** (2 districts).
- **Southern Boundary:** **Madhya Pradesh** (Touches **11 Districts:** Agra, Etawah, Jalaun, Jhansi, Lalitpur, Mahoba, Banda, Chitrakoot, Prayagraj, Mirzapur, Sonbhadra — longest inter-state border).
- **South-Eastern Boundary:** **Chhattisgarh** (Touches only **Sonbhadra**) & **Jharkhand** (Touches only **Sonbhadra**).
- **Eastern Boundary:** **Bihar** (Touches 7 Districts: Sonbhadra, Chandauli, Ghazipur, Ballia, Deoria, Kushinagar, Maharajganj).

> **Four District Extremes:**  
> - **Northernmost District:** **Saharanpur** (surrounded by Haryana, HP, Uttarakhand)  
> - **Southernmost District:** **Sonbhadra** (**Only district in India bordering 4 states**: MP, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Bihar)  
> - **Easternmost District:** **Ballia** (wedged into Bihar)  
> - **Westernmost District:** **Shamli**  
> - **Largest District by Area:** **Lakhimpur Kheri** (7,680 sq km)  
> - **Smallest District by Area:** **Hapur** (660 sq km)

---

## 2. यूपी की जल निकासी प्रणाली, नदियाँ, नहरें और आर्द्रभूमियाँ (Drainage System, Rivers, Canals & Wetlands of UP)

### A. Major Rivers of Uttar Pradesh
| नदी | Entry Point in UP | Exit Point / Confluence | Total Districts in UP & Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ganga** | Enters at **Bijnor** | Exits UP at **Ballia** into Bihar | Traverses **28 districts** (~1,450 km in UP). Receives Ramganga at Kannauj, Yamuna at Prayagraj, Gomti at Saidpur (Ghazipur), Ghaghara at Chhapra (Bihar border). |
| **Yamuna** | Enters at **Saharanpur** (Faizabad village) | Joins Ganga at **Prayagraj (Triveni Sangam)** | Traverses **19 districts**. Receives Hindon at Noida, Chambal at Pachnada (Etawah), Betwa at Hamirpur, Ken at Chilla (Banda). |
| **Ramganga** | Enters at **Bijnor** (near Kalagarh) | Joins Ganga near **Kannauj** | Passes through Corbett National Park; flows via Moradabad, Bareilly. |
| **Gomti** | Originates at **Fulhar Lake (Panghaili Jheel), Pilibhit** | Joins Ganga near Saidpur, Ghazipur | **Only major Himalayan tributary originating in the plains of UP**. Lucknow, Sultanpur, Jaunpur situated on its banks. |
| **Ghaghara (Saryu)** | Enters near Kheri/Bahraich | Joins Ganga at Chhapra (Bihar) | Originates at Mapchachungo glacier (Tibet); called Karnali in Nepal, Saryu at Ayodhya. |
| **Betwa (Vetravati)** | Enters at **Lalitpur** | Joins Yamuna at **Hamirpur** | Originates in Vindhyan range (MP); Rajghat, Parichha, and Matatila dams built on it. |
| **Ken (Karnavati)** | Enters at **Banda** | Joins Yamuna at **Chilla (Banda)** | Ken-Betwa river interlinking project is India's first river link project. |
| **Son** | Enters and exits in **Sonbhadra** | Joins Ganga near Patna | Originates at Amarkantak plateau (MP). |

### B. Major Canals & Reservoirs
- **Sharda Canal:** Longest canal network in Uttar Pradesh (originates from Sharda river at Banbasa, India-Nepal border).
- **Upper Ganga Canal:** Constructed by Sir Proby Cautley (1854); originates at Haridwar (Bhimoda).
- **Govind Ballabh Pant Sagar:** **Largest artificial lake in India**, formed by the **Rihand Dam** on Rihand river at Pipri in **Sonbhadra**.
- **Parichha Dam & Matatila Dam:** Built on **Betwa river** (Jhansi / Lalitpur).
- **Keetham Lake (Sur Sarovar):** Ramsar site in **Agra**.

### C. All 10 Ramsar Wetland Sites in Uttar Pradesh
1. **Upper Ganga River (Brijghat to Narora Stretch):** First Ramsar site in UP (designated 2005).
2. **Nawabganj Bird Sanctuary** (Unnao) - renamed Shahid Chandra Shekhar Azad Bird Sanctuary.
3. **Parvati Arga Bird Sanctuary** (Gonda) - oxbow lake.
4. **Saman Bird Sanctuary** (Mainpuri).
5. **Samaspur Bird Sanctuary** (Rae Bareli).
6. **Sandi Bird Sanctuary** (Hardoi).
7. **Sarsai Nawar Jheel** (Etawah) - sarus crane habitat.
8. **Sur Sarovar (Keetham Lake)** (Agra).
9. **Haiderpur Wetland** (Muzaffarnagar & Bijnor, designated 2021).
10. **Bakhira Wildlife Sanctuary** (Sant Kabir Nagar, designated 2022).

---

## 3. राष्ट्रीय उद्यान, बाघ अभयारण्य और वन्यजीव अभयारण्य (National Parks, Tiger Reserves & Wildlife Sanctuaries)

- **Only National Park in UP:** **Dudhwa National Park** (Lakhimpur Kheri, established 1977). Famous for Swamp Deer (Barasingha) and One-horned Rhinoceros reintroduction.
- **Tiger Reserves in UP (4 Total):**
  1. **Dudhwa Tiger Reserve** (Lakhimpur Kheri - declared 1987)
  2. **Pilibhit Tiger Reserve** (Pilibhit - declared 2014; won TX2 global award)
  3. **Amangarh Tiger Reserve** (Bijnor - buffer zone of Jim Corbett NP retained in UP after UK partition)
  4. **Ranipur Tiger Reserve** (**Chitrakoot** - declared 2022 as UP's 4th and India's 53rd Tiger Reserve, in Bundelkhand region).
- **Elephant Reserve:** Terai Elephant Reserve (TER) established in Lakhimpur Kheri/Pilibhit belt (2022).
- **Hastinapur Wildlife Sanctuary:** **Largest wildlife sanctuary in UP** (spread across Meerut, Muzzafarnagar, Bijnor, Amroha, Hapur).
- **Mahavir Swami Wildlife Sanctuary:** **Smallest wildlife sanctuary in UP** (Lalitpur, ~5.4 sq km).
- **Kachhua (Turtle) Sanctuary:** Varanasi (now shifted to Bhadohi/Prayagraj stretch).
- **State Symbols:** Bird: **Sarus Crane** (*Grus antigone*); Animal: **Swamp Deer** (Barasingha); Tree: **Ashoka**; Flower: **Palash / Dhak** (*Butea monosperma* - Flame of the Forest); Aquatic Animal: **Gangetic Dolphin** (*Platanista gangetica*).

---

## 4. यूपी के 9 कृषि-जलवायु क्षेत्र और कृषि (9 Agro-Climatic Zones & Agriculture of UP)

1. **Tarai Zone:** Bhabhar and Terai belt along foothills (high rainfall, sugarcane, paddy).
2. **Western Plain Zone:** Meerut division, high agricultural productivity (Green Revolution heartland).
3. **Mid-Western Plain Zone:** Bareilly & Moradabad divisions.
4. **South-Western Semi-Arid Zone:** Agra, Mathura, Aligarh (low rainfall, mustard, bajra).
5. **Central Plain Zone:** Lucknow, Kanpur, Ayodhya divisions (alluvial doab).
6. **Bundelkhand Zone:** 7 districts (Jhansi, Lalitpur, Jalaun, Hamirpur, Mahoba, Banda, Chitrakoot) — semi-arid, rocky terrain, pulses, oilseeds.
7. **North-Eastern Plain Zone:** Gorakhpur division (floods, sugarcane, rice).
8. **Eastern Plain Zone:** Varanasi, Azamgarh, Ghazipur, Jaunpur divisions.
9. **Vindhyan Zone:** Mirzapur, Sonbhadra, and southern Chandauli (plateau, red soil, coarse grains).

> **UP Agricultural Ranks in India:**  
> - **Rank 1 in:** Total Foodgrain production, Wheat, Sugarcane, Potato, Milk, Mango, Guava, Ethanol production.  
> - **Rank 2 in:** Rice (after West Bengal).

---

## 5. उत्तर प्रदेश में खनिज एवं औद्योगिक बेल्ट (Minerals & Industrial Belts in Uttar Pradesh)

- Uttar Pradesh is generally poor in metallic minerals; mineral wealth is concentrated in the southern plateau districts (Bundelkhand & Vindhyan region):
  - **Uranium:** **Lalitpur** (only district in UP with Uranium occurrences)
  - **Copper:** **Sonrai area, Lalitpur**
  - **Limestone:** **Sonbhadra** (Gurma, Kanach, Kajrahat) & **Mirzapur** (supplies raw material for cement factories at Churk, Dalla, Chunar)
  - **Glass Sand (Silica Sand):** **Shankargarh & Lohgara (Prayagraj)**, Chitrakoot, Banda (derived from disintegration of Vindhyan sandstones)
  - **Bauxite (Aluminium ore):** **Banda, Chitrakoot, Chandauli** (supplies Renukoot Hindalco smelter)
  - **Coal:** **Singrauli (Sonbhadra)** — power capital of India (NTPC plants)
  - **Dolomite:** **Sonbhadra & Banda**
  - **Pyrophyllite & Diaspore:** **Jhansi, Lalitpur, Mahoba, Hamirpur**
  - **China Clay:** **Sonbhadra & Banda**
  - **Gold:** Small placer deposits in **Sharda and Ramganga river sands**; Hardi village in Sonbhadra.

---

## 6. उत्तर प्रदेश में 1857 का विद्रोह: केंद्र एवं नेता (Revolt of 1857 in Uttar Pradesh: Centres & Leaders)

| Centre in UP | Indian Leader / Revolutionary | British Officer / Suppressor |
|---|---|---|
| **मेरठ** | Sepoys of 3rd Native Cavalry (10 May 1857) | General Hewitt |
| **Delhi / Western UP** | General Bakht Khan (from Bareilly) | John Nicholson, Hudson |
| **कानपुर** | **Nana Saheb (Dhondu Pant)**, Tantia Tope (Ramchandra Pandurang), Azimullah Khan | Colin Campbell, Havelock |
| **लखनऊ** | **Begum Hazrat Mahal** (on behalf of minor son Birjis Qadr) | Colin Campbell, Outram, Havelock |
| **झाँसी** | **Rani Laxmi Bai (Manikarnika)** | **Sir Hugh Rose** (*"Here lay the woman who was the only man among the rebels"*) |
| **बरेली** | **Khan Bahadur Khan** | Vincent Eyre, Colin Campbell |
| **Faizabad (Ayodhya)** | **मौलवी अहमदुल्ला शाह** (*डंका शाह*) (**Maulvi Ahmadullah Shah** (*Danka Shah*)) | Campbell |
| **Prayagraj (Allahabad)** | **मौलवी लियाकत अली** (**Maulvi Liaquat Ali**) | Colonel James Neill |
| **Jagdishpur / East UP** | Kunwar Singh & Amar Singh | William Taylor, Vincent Eyre |

## 7. सांस्कृतिक घराने, लोक संगीत और नृत्य (Cultural Gharanas, Folk Music & Dances)

- **Kathak:** Only Classical Dance of North India. Reached its zenith under **Nawab Wajid Ali Shah** of Awadh.
  - **Lucknow Gharana exponents:** Thakur Prasad, Kalka-Bindadin Maharaj, Achchan Maharaj, Shambhu Maharaj, Lachhu Maharaj, **Pt. Birju Maharaj**.
  - **Banaras Gharana exponents:** Sitara Devi, Pt. Kishan Maharaj (Tabla), Pt. Samta Prasad (Gudai Maharaj - Tabla), Bismillah Khan (Shehnai - Bharat Ratna 2001).
  - **Kirana Gharana:** Founded by **Ustad Abdul Karim Khan & Abdul Wahid Khan** in Muzaffarnagar district (Bhimsen Joshi, Gangubai Hangal belonged to this tradition).
  - **Atrali Gharana:** Aligarh.
- **Folk Music Genres:**
  - **Rasiya:** Braj region (Mathura, Vrindavan) — themed on Radha-Krishna romance.
  - **Kajri:** Mirzapur & Varanasi region — sung during monsoon / rainy season.
  - **Birha:** Eastern UP (Bhojpuri belt) — heroic and narrative folklore.
  - **Alha:** Bundelkhand region (Mahoba) — martial epic commemorating heroic deeds of Alha & Udal.
  - **Chaiti & Hori:** Purvanchal & Braj region.
- **Folk Dances of UP:**
  - **Charkula Dance:** **Braj region** — women balance a multi-tiered wooden pyramid with 108 oil lamps on their heads.
  - **Karma Dance:** **Sonbhadra & Mirzapur** (performed by tribal communities like Kharwar).
  - **Pai Danda & Diwari:** **Bundelkhand** (stick dance reminiscent of Raas Leela).
  - **Nautanki:** Traditional folk theatre of UP (Hathras & Kanpur schools).
